```rust
    type State = JitRequest;                       // the tier's own, laid with the request (§2.2, revision 66)
    #[inline(always)]
    fn safepoint(req: &mut Request, frame: FrameRef, site: Site, held: Held<'_>) -> Flow { dispatch::safepoint(req, frame, site, held) }
    fn lay(started: &Started<'_>) -> Option<JitRequest> { JitRequest::laid(started) }   // the engine state's `Shared`, once per request
    fn extension() -> Option<Extension> {
        Some(Extension::new("jit")
            .ini(policy::INI)                      // php's opcache.jit* names, plus jit.mode and jit.compile
            .engine_state(JitEngine::new))         // fn(&StartupIni) -> JitEngine
    }
}
pub(crate) struct Shared { policy: Policy, backend: Option<Backend>, started: Box<[Note]>, told: AtomicBool, perf: Outputs }   // the table is the epoch's (§4.5, revision 27); no log (revision 34)
pub(crate) struct JitEngine { compiler: Compiler, shared: Arc<Shared> }            // field order: the worker is joined before the table drops
pub struct JitRequest {                    // php's JIT_G, read by `tier::state` (revision 66)
    shared: Arc<Shared>,                   // the engine state, resolved once as the request is laid
    submitter: Submitter,                  // where its recordings compile: the engine's worker and queue, or inline (§4.8)
    recording: Cell<bool>,                 // php's JIT_G(tracing): the request's recording exclusion, set and cleared by record's guard alone;
                                           // no fiber switch saves it (revision 30)
    submitted: Cell<Option<Submitted>>,    // the trace it submitted to the worker and has not reached (§4.8)
}
```

`JitEngine` holds no lock of its own; the one lock is inside `TraceTable`, and the compiler's queue is a second, private one that no safepoint takes (§4.8). `Shared` exists because the worker thread needs the policy, the backend and a sink by `Arc` and `JitEngine` is owned by the engine, not the other way round, and it reaches the table through the epoch its job's claim holds (revision 27). It holds no log: a root's lines after its start are written by the pipeline to the request it runs on, and a `jit_debug` that asks for one compiles inline (§4.8, revision 34), so the worker writes none. When `Backend::new` fails at `JitEngine::new`, or `opcache.jit_buffer_size` is zero, or `opcache.jit` is `disable` or a setting php refuses, or opcache does not start for the host and no `jit.mode` names a lane (`opcache.enable` off, or on the command line and in phpdbg `opcache.enable_cli` off, revision 44), the tier runs with `Mode::Off`: the engine binds `Nop`'s loop and does not ask it (below), no worker is spawned, `backend` is `None` and never read, and `opcache.jit_debug` is read as asking for nothing, so no line is logged: php never starts its JIT where opcache does not start (`accel_startup`, `Z/ext/opcache/ZendAccelerator.c:3210-3220`), nor under `opcache.jit=disable`, a refused setting or a zero buffer, so no bit is read and no line written whatever they ask (`gh13232.phpt`) (revision 39). Under `opcache.jit=off` with a buffer php starts its JIT and traces only what a request persists after turning it on (§4.7), so the tier runs as tracing, its worker spawned, its `Backend` built and its `opcache.jit_debug` bits read for the traces such a unit grows. `TraceTable::new` does not fail: an arena that cannot be mapped, as on a target with no `Pages` adapter yet, is none, and each publish into the epoch's table numbers its trace under the same bounds and keeps no code, so its word reads compiled and every reader takes it as not (revisions 27, 34). Under `Mode::Off` the engine binds `Nop`'s loop and never asks the tier: `Jit::bound` answers the engine's start with `tier::Bound`, `Never` where php's tier can never start (opcache unstarted, `disable`, a zero buffer, a refusal, no `Backend`), `Runtime` under `opcache.jit=off` with a buffer, which a request may turn on (§4.7), and `On` where it runs from the start. `On` binds `Jit`'s loop, whose safepoint is outlined. `Runtime` binds both loops, as ADR-0006's amendment on #3301 rules: the engine keeps them in its `tier::Binding`, and a request keeps the loops it is served in in its `tier::TierState`, which every serving it starts takes, an `execute`, a re-entry and a resumption alike. A request starts in `Jit`'s loops once any unit with counting words (§4.7) has been published in the engine, and in `Nop`'s otherwise, so an engine set up and off whose requests switch nothing on pays at a safepoint what an engine without the tier pays. A request in `Nop`'s loop that publishes a unit with counting words raises its own interrupt, and at the next check that can take it, a frame's activation or a back edge, `Nop`'s loop lays the request's tier again with `Jit`'s loops and the state they lay and hands its frame to `Jit`'s loop, which serves the rest of that serving on the entry slot it stands on, nested under `Nop`'s on the machine stack (`tier::interrupted_at`, #3362). `Nop`'s serving is then over: the site leaves along the `Threw` edge it leaves along for a hook's throwable, and `unwind_frame` answers `Done` for it, so the site's code keeps main's shape. The activation's frame is handed as pushed, so `Jit`'s loop raises its `Site::Entry`. A check that cannot take it, after a call or outside the loop, raises the interrupt again for the next. No safepoint or opline tests the switch. Coarser than php, as #3301 accepts: once a request is in `Jit`'s loops a sealed unit's safepoints pay the outlined call, and a request already in flight stays in `Nop`'s until its next start. Of a request in flight, as #3510 accepts (revision 123): a serving that stood below the one that took the check, suspended in a native or engine call, a callback's caller or an autoloader's, serves on in `Nop`'s loop to its end, its calls into the unit uncounted for this request, though every serving started after the hand-off is `Jit`'s; it reaches only requests in flight at the engine's first counting publication, both loops run the same handlers, and the registry records it (`trace:tier-standing-serving-stays`). G1 measured the per-safepoint `req.engine::<JitEngine>()` lookup this replaces (#3133; revision 59). The tier is not an extension: it is not under `ext/*`, does not depend on `php_ext`, and is not in the bundle's list. It uses the extension mechanism for its state because design §4.4 is the one mechanism for per-engine and per-request state and a second would fail its own deletion test.

The lock, stated where the rule lives. Design §4.4's "`E` may not contain a lock" names the trace table and the compiler's queue as the two engine-owned exceptions beside the unit cache; both are reached through the tier's own state and never through an extension's.

### 4.1 `record`

```rust
pub(crate) enum Start { Loop, Enter, Return, Side { parent: TraceId, exit: ExitId } }
pub(crate) struct TraceRecord { start: Start, root: FuncRef, events: Vec<Event>, stop: Stop }
pub(crate) struct Recorded { record: TraceRecord, view: UnitView }
pub(crate) enum Event {
    Op { func: FuncRef, opline: OplineIdx, op1: Sample, op2: Sample, data: Sample, branch: Option<bool> },   // whether a conditional jump, or the one a fused compare-and-branch serves, was taken (revision 26)
    InitCall { callee: Callee, began: Began },   // Callee: User(FuncRef), Native(Types), what a native declares it returns (revision 75); Began: Here, Before, php's FAKE_INIT_CALL (revision 72)
    Enter { func: FuncRef },                 // a PHP-to-PHP call followed
    Back,                                    // its return
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy)] pub(crate) struct Sample { tag: Tag, packed: bool, reference: bool, indirect: bool, class: Option<ClassId> }
pub(crate) enum Stop { Loop, Link(TraceId), Return, RecursiveCall, RecursiveRet, Interpreter, Trampoline, PropHookCall, BadFunc,
    NotSupported, Exception, TooLong, TooDeep, TooDeepRet, DeepRecursion, LoopUnroll, LoopExit, RecursionExit, InnerLoop, CompiledLoop,
    TooManyTraces, TooManyChildren, TooManyExits, CompilerError, MovedFrame, RequestUnit }
pub(crate) fn run(req: &mut Request, frame: FrameRef, start: Start) -> Result<Recorded, Bailout>;
impl TraceRecord { pub(crate) fn dump(&self) -> String; pub(crate) fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self, ParseError>; }
```

`run` is one loop. Before each opline it samples the three operands from the frame, following `IS_INDIRECT` and `IS_REFERENCE` and marking both, and pushes one `Op`; then it decides whether to step at all: it stops with `NotSupported` at `CATCH`, `FAST_CALL`, `FAST_RET`, with `Interpreter` at megamorphic calls and `INCLUDE_OR_EVAL`, with `MovedFrame` on a heap frame. It reads the `Func` word at the loop header, entry or return it has reached (the word is at the number the opline carries, or number zero for an entry): `compiled` at a loop header inside the trace stops with `CompiledLoop`, and `compiled` at a root it reaches stops with `Link(id)`, as php does at a handler that is a trace (§A.4); the id becomes the `link` of the trace's last exit (§4.2). A fused compare-and-branch is one `step` and one `Op`, with the direction taken in `branch`, as php's record keeps the branch on the comparison (unverified against `zend_jit_trace_record`); every other conditional jump's `Op` carries its direction too, since php reads where its trace went from the next record and from its `END` record's opline, which this record has not (revision 26). Then it calls `step` and matches:

| `Stepped` | The recorder |
|---|---|
| `Same(f)` | continues at `f`; at the root opline after a `Loop` start, closes the trace |
| `Pushed(f)` | tries `view.hold(req, f.func())`; a request-scoped op array cannot be held, and the recording stops there with `Interpreter`, as php's `zend_jit_trace_execute` stops at a frame whose op array keeps no jit extension (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_vm_helpers.c:1097-1109`): the trace leaves at the `DO_*CALL`, whose handler activates the callee, and arms the landing past it; a return a `Return` start follows into a request-scoped frame stops with `Return` on the same branch, and `RequestUnit` is left to a root the view cannot hold; a record names no request-scoped op array, the callee of an `InitCall` it writes for one being the request's and no number (design §3.4); a callee whose op array is a property hook's body stops with `PropHookCall`, as php's recorder stops at a callee with `prop_info` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_vm_helpers.c:1129-1132`, revision 122); otherwise pushes `Enter { func: f.func() }`, depth + 1, and reads `f`'s entry word |
| `Popped(f)` | pushes `Back`, depth − 1. At depth zero the root frame returned: for a `Loop` or `Enter` start the trace ends after it, and the lowering places php's IP guard on the unknown caller (§A.5); a `Return` start roots at the caller's landing past the call (§2.2), so its depth-zero `Popped` follows the return past its root only where php's recursive return does, behind the IP guard, and otherwise ends the trace (revision 61) |
| `Moved(f)` | stops with `MovedFrame` |
| `Threw(f)` | stops with `Exception`; `hot` hands `f` back to `execute` through `Resume` |
| `Done` | cannot occur: see below |

Calls begun before the recording (revision 72). A recording that starts in a frame with calls its `INIT_*` began and no `DO_*CALL` has made yet, the records above it on the stack (`tier::under_way`), writes each down first, the outermost first, as `InitCall { began: Before }`, php's `ZEND_JIT_TRACE_FAKE_INIT_CALL` (`zend_jit_trace_record_fake_init_call`, `Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_vm_helpers.c:631-682`), and so does a return it follows past its root into a frame with calls under way, as php's does at `:1189-1196`. The trace's sends and its `DO_*CALL` serve those calls as any other; compiled code reads such a call's room where the interpreter stands, `Stood.call`, which `enter` lays down for a trace that reads it before any kernel stood the frame.

Where a loop is rooted (revision 49). A `Loop` start roots at the opline php's trace roots at, the loop's header php's CFG finds, which `zend_jit_setup_hot_trace_counters` marks `ZEND_JIT_TRACE_START_LOOP` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:8963-8980`): for a `for` or a `while`, compiled with a `JMP` to its condition before its body, the condition, and for every other loop the opline its back edge lands on (`record::loop_root`). php's CFG is built over the op array its optimizer left, so a condition pass 1 folds as it is written is read as folded: a `while (true)` or a `for (;;)`, whose condition is a `JMPZ` or `JMPNZ` on a constant operand that takes it back to the body, which php's pass 1 makes a `JMP` (`Z/Zend/Optimizer/pass1.c:315-333`) and its block pass threads the `JMP` into the loop through (`Z/Zend/Optimizer/block_pass.c:1272-1295`), is rooted at its body, and its condition is no header, so the opline past a call that ends the body is a landing a trace arms (revision 88, #3317). It is read as folded where `opcache.optimization_level` runs the passes that fold it, pass 5, or pass 1 with pass 3 (`Z/Zend/Optimizer/zend_optimizer.c:1072-1107`), which php's default level does; at a level that runs neither, php's CFG heads such a loop at its condition, the `while`'s line, as it heads a `for`'s, and so does the recorder (`record::Folds`, revision 121, #3578). The back edge's `Site::Loop` stands the frame on the body, so the recording, and the entry of the trace its word names, step the rest of the body to the condition through `step` first (`record::headed`), and a body that leaves the loop on the way, a `break`, a return or a throw, records and enters nothing and resumes where it went. A trace rooted at a condition keeps a second way in at the body, `types::BodyEntry`, which the back edge enters with the frame held to what the trace knows there and which declines, with no exit, to that step where it holds anything else (revision 53). A loop whose condition is one fused compare over variables and constants is recorded from behind it instead, the condition written down as the back edge served it and the body recorded on from where the frame stands, with nothing stepped (`record::behind`, revision 54). Both are kept as built and ADR-0006 is not amended for an engine site at the condition, as Mitchell ruled on #3178 (revision 70): php puts its loop counter on the header by swapping that opline's handler, which an op array byte-identical to php's and shared immutable (design §3.4) cannot take, so such a site would cost a test in every compare handler or a mutable handler table. A condition the fused compare does not cover, a call or a `TMP` operand, still steps one body through `record::headed` (#3204). A loop header met inside a recording is that opline (`record::header_at`), so a trace closes, links and stops at an inner loop where php's does. A loop php's CFG reaches only through a `catch` is no loop php counts: `zend_build_cfg` draws no edge from a `try` into its `catch`, so such code has no immediate dominator and `zend_cfg_identify_loops` finds no header in it (`Z/Zend/Optimizer/zend_cfg.c:694-724`, `:832-844`), and `zend_jit_setup_hot_trace_counters` never swaps its handler (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:8954-8980`); its root is not reached from the entry along php's edges (`record::found`), so it is no header a recording meets, and its back edge's first claim blacklists its word with no line (`record::counts`, revision 90, #3201). Nor is a loop php counts whose every jump back to it is one the entry does not reach, as php's CFG draws no edge from a block the entry does not reach (`zend_cfg_build_predecessors`, `Z/Zend/Optimizer/zend_cfg.c:607-617`): a `foreach` whose body always returns, which a recording goes through as php's does (revision 101, #3438).

The rule about an entry frame, stated with the stack's slot: an opline that leaves its frame, a `RETURN*`, a `YIELD*` or a `GENERATOR_CREATE` (revision 42), at depth zero is stepped only when the root frame is not `req.stack().entry()`, because when it is, the caller is native and there is nothing to follow; and a `Return` start never starts on an entry frame because `Site::Return` is never raised there (§2.2). So `Done` is unreachable from the recorder, and the arm is `unreachable!()` with that sentence beside it. Before stepping the recorder reads nothing it needs afterwards, because `Stepped` carries the frame to continue in and the arm says what became of the old one. A native call that re-enters the interpreter through a nested `execute` returns `Same` and is opaque, as php's `DO_ICALL` is; whatever that nested `execute` did at its own safepoints, it did not record, because the request's exclusion, php's `JIT_G(tracing)`, is set for the length of this `run`, on whichever execution the nested `execute` runs, a fiber's included (§4.9, revision 30). After each step it checks the length limit and stops with `TooLong`. A `Bailout` from `step` ends recording with no record and is the `Err` of `run`. The buffer is php's flat record (`zend_jit_internal.h:365-373`), one `Vec` bounded by `opcache.jit_max_trace_length`.

The owned view. `record::run` builds `Recorded.view` as it goes through the tier face's builders (§5.3): `hold(req, f)` accepts an op array whose unit is published and returns `false` for a request-scoped one, holding nothing. `Recorded.view` holds the request's epoch once. `hold_class(req, c)` holds nothing and accepts a class whose `ClassId` is published (`ClassId::is_published`), since a published row lives as long as the epoch (design §5.14 *Identities*). A request-scoped class is one whose `ClassId` is request-local: its link read a request-owned class, or the cache did not hold its unit (design §6.2). It is recorded as its id and guarded through the request's cache slot, and `UnitView::class` is defined only for a class `hold_class` accepted. As built (revision 93, #3337; revision 97, #3346), a request-scoped class's place is taken with its cache slot (`UnitView::hold_place`, `PropertySlot::cached`), so its property oplines are compiled in place under the guard through that slot (§4.3's *Objects*), and its id is never baked into a trace. `hold_frameless(req, n)` takes what the native of row `n` of the engine's frameless table declares it returns, by which `types` types a frameless call's result, as php's trace types it through the call map it builds for every op array a trace runs through, a file's own code among them (`zend_get_func_info`, `Z/Zend/Optimizer/zend_func_info.c:132-150`), and as it types a native's `DO_ICALL`'s by the record's callee (revision 101, #3438). `UnitView` is therefore a value that holds the request's epoch and the ids it accepted, and is `Send`; `types::build` needs nothing else, and runs on the compiler's thread (§4.8).

What recording costs, per opline, is one call into `step` and the `match` it runs; G7 reports it.

Tested: `dump` and `parse` are inverses, so a recorded trace is a fixture; `run` runs on a real request under `Capture` with `jit.mode=force`, and its `TRACE start/stop` lines are diffed against the oracle's on the seven kernels through `oracle::compare` with the divergence registry (`docs/divergence.toml`, kind `trace`). Three differences from php's traces are decided here, ahead of the recorder: a trace whose root is the entry frame of a nested `execute` stops with `Return` one opline earlier than php's (whether php's does the same at a native caller is unverified); when the root table fills this engine blacklists the one root that hit `Full` where php stops every counter handler at once (`zend_jit_stop_counter_handlers`, §A.4); and a typed function's call whose `RECV` throws is a root in php, whose counter is on opline zero, and not here, where `Site::Entry` is raised only after the prologue ran without raising (design §5.6, ledger 9). The third is a registry row today, because the interpreter already raises `Site::Entry` after the prologue. The first two have no engine half until `record` and `table` exist, and each enters the registry as an `open` row the first time the `TRACE` diff raises it, for a person to accept against that reading. The recorder's classification is a table over `Stepped`, and each arm is a fixture that drives `step` on a witness which takes that edge (a call, a return, a `yield` that creates a generator, a throw, a return trace, a `do { } while (!$x)` loop, a fused branch taken each way).

### 4.2 `types`

```rust
pub(crate) struct TraceIr { entry: Entry, frames: Vec<FrameInfo>, values: Vec<Value>, ops: Vec<Op>, guards: Vec<Guard>, exits: Vec<ExitSpec>, map_ptrs: Vec<MapPtrRaw>, end: End }
pub(crate) struct Value { slot: Slot, types: Types, def: Def, home: Home }   // Def: Entry, Phi { entry, back }, Op(OpId), Reload(OpId); Home: Memory, Register
pub(crate) enum Op { Line(Line), InitCall { frame, callee, began }, Enter { frame }, Back { frame, out, escapes } }   // Line: the opline, its operands and writes, the jump it is fused to (Fused::{Jmpz, Jmpnz}), its immediate (Imm::{Arg, Binary}), Form::{Inline, Step}, its own exit where an inline form cannot finish, and its unwind where a kernel it calls throws (revision 28; revision 45)
pub(crate) struct Guard { at: GuardAt, check: Check, exit: ExitId }   // GuardAt: Entry, Before(OpId), Within(OpId); Check: Type, Deref, Layout, Direction, Stands (php's IP guard, revision 41), Arm (a table's jump's target, revision 73), Room (php's stack check, revision 73)
pub(crate) struct ExitSpec { frame: Frame, opline: Option<OplineIdx>, landing: Landing, stores: Vec<Store>, known: Vec<Known>, grows: Grows, link: Option<TraceId>, layout: bool }   // one exit per spec that names an opline and is equal with its frame named by its op array, php's reuse (revision 73); layout: php's ZEND_JIT_EXIT_PACKED_GUARD; Stored: Value, Copied { value, read: InPlace | Through } (revision 35; revision 45); Known { slot, tag }, php's STACK_TYPE; Grows: Side, Never (ZEND_JIT_EXIT_TO_VM)
pub(crate) struct Unwind { stores: Vec<Store> }   // where a throw leaves, taking no exit: php's exception stub (revision 45)
#[derive(Clone, Copy)] pub(crate) struct Landing { frees: Frees /* op1, op2 */, restores_call: bool }   // two facets, as php's ZEND_JIT_EXIT_FREE_OP1/OP2 and ZEND_JIT_EXIT_RESTORE_CALL are (revision 101)
pub(crate) enum End { Loop(LoopHeader /* at: OpId, repeat: Loop | Call | Return, interrupt: Interrupt */), Leave(ExitId) }
pub(crate) enum Interrupt { Exit(ExitId), Stub(OplineIdx), None }   // php's ZEND_JIT_EXIT_TO_VM exit point, or its jit_stub_interrupt_handler, which takes none (revision 51), or no test, a recursive return's (revision 87)
#[derive(Clone, Copy)] pub(crate) struct ExitId(u16);
pub(crate) struct Abort { stop: Stop, why: &'static str }
pub(crate) fn build(rec: &TraceRecord, view: &UnitView) -> Result<TraceIr, Abort>;
```

Pure: a record in, an SSA over frame slots out, with php's guard set placed where a recorded type is narrower than what the op array alone implies (§E.5), aliased variables kept at guard strength (§A.4), `SMART_BRANCH` refused into one compare-and-branch whose mispredict exit lands on the opline the jump goes to the other way, where php's lands (§E.5; revision 50), and an `ExitSpec` per guard naming the slots that must be stored and the `Landing`. `End::Loop` names the position the back edge jumps to, for php's three stops that make a loop (`LOOP`, `RECURSIVE_CALL`, `RECURSIVE_RET`), and where its interrupt test leaves, or that a loop by recursive return takes none, as php's takes none (revision 87); the lowering puts the test there (§4.3). A trace that does not loop ends through `End::Leave`'s exit (revision 26). `Landing` is decided at build time (whether a temporary is live at the exit opline is a fact about the op array; whether an `INIT_FCALL` sequence is open is a fact about the record), and a link is a build-time fact on the exit a `Link` stop ends on. **The clobber rule.** A kernel call whose opcode carries the `call` effect in `ops.toml` may run user code, and user code may write any array, object or reference the trace holds a derived fact about; after such a call every memory-derived value in the lattice (an array's length or packed flag, a slot's type where the slot is reachable through a reference or a symbol table, a property offset's class) is reloaded or re-guarded, and only register-resident scalars the trace itself owns survive. A kernel with the `throw` effect and no `call` effect clobbers nothing but may exit. `Abort` is the recoverable half of `Stop` and counts toward the blacklist: it carries the stop it counts as, the record's own where it stopped on a failed attempt, php's `TOO_MANY_EXITS` past `ZEND_JIT_TRACE_MAX_EXITS`, and `CompilerError` where the record does not fit the op arrays its view holds (revision 26).

*As built* (revision 26, #2954). Every slot of every frame the trace touches holds one value at a time, typed by php's `zend_update_type_info` over the `MAY_BE_*` bits for the scalar core and anything past it; what a slot entered with is, in a trace that enters its function, a parameter its declaration (`UnitView::parameters`, php's `arg_info`) and any other variable unset, and elsewhere what php's analysis knows reaches the opline the frame's part begins at, met for a side with what its parent's exit knew (revisions 89 and 124), and a variable no opline makes a reference none. A guard is held once at entry for a value the root's frame entered with, by the inline op that computed it for an arithmetic's overflow and a fetched element's type, as php's code generator holds a guarded value it writes, within a `FETCH_OBJ_R` its handler serves where php's code generator fetches the property inline into a register (revision 140), and before the first op php's code generator reads it at otherwise, `CHECK_OP_TRACE_TYPE` over the mark the guard leaves on each of the value's reads, an op before that guard reading the value as it was made (`Value::unguarded`, revision 144); a loop's phi guard moves to its entry value where the back edge already has the type, or where what the back edge brings is made by an inline `ADD`, `SUB`, `MUL`, `+=`, `-=`, `*=`, increment or decrement over numbers, which then holds it long within itself and reruns where it overflows, php's `is_checked_guard` (revision 47), and not where a line after it reads the variable and may take a counted owner of it, an array's element or a sent argument among them, since php's trace SSA numbers the variable anew there under `ZEND_SSA_RC_INFERENCE` and the back edge brings that line's value, which `is_checked_guard` holds nothing of (revision 133), and not where such a line comes before the read the guard is held at, since the guard is then php's on the number that line made, which takes `MAY_BE_GUARD` in the phi's stead, so the guard stays at the read and takes its exit there, and a phi a type guard holds within the loop's body stays in its slot (revision 136); an inline `ADD` or `SUB` of the constant `1` held long leaves past itself where it overflows instead, with the double it went to in its result and known one, as php's `zend_jit_math_long_long` takes that exit at `opline + 1` with the constant in its stack map (`Stored::Overflowed`), the site writing it in its own frame and the exit's reuse naming the slot by its frame alone, as php's stack map is frame-relative, an exit exit-fuzz never takes, since it is right only where the op overflowed (revision 132); so does an increment or a decrement held long, with its variable the double and its result, where it is used, the same double for a `PRE_*` and the long the variable held for a `POST_*` (`Stored::Limit`), each known as what it is, as php's `zend_jit_inc_dec` takes that exit (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:4775-4810`, revision 135); and an increment or a decrement held a double, since its record went past the limit, leaves past itself where it does not overflow, with its variable the long it went to and its result, where it is used, that long for a `PRE_*` and the long the variable held for a `POST_*` (`Stored::Stayed`), each known a long and written by the site in its own frame, as `zend_jit_inc_dec` takes that exit with the step's register in its stack map (`:4824-4856`, revision 137); and an increment or a decrement takes none of these exits where php's `zend_may_overflow` says it cannot overflow, which reads the range its operand holds as the code generator compiles it, not as the trace SSA's dump shows it: a comparison compiled as the trace's branch leaves what it read bounded on the way the trace goes on (`zend_jit_trace_update_condition_ranges`), and each long an opline defines is propagated again over what its operands hold once it is compiled, a variable the trace SSA numbers anew among them, so a loop's `$i < $n` bounds the `$i++` after it below `ZEND_LONG_MAX` unless a send of `$i` the propagation knows nothing of comes between (revision 139). A long or a double that a `FETCH_OBJ_R` the tier steps writes and an op reads, where `Builder::supports_reg` says both keep it in a register as `zend_jit_trace_allocate_registers` keeps it (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:2958-3058`), is held within the fetch by two exits past it, in place and through the reference it may be, as `zend_jit_fetch_obj` holds what it reads into a register by `zend_jit_guard_fetch_result_type` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:14607-14687`, `:8024-8090`), not before the reading op by one; the two read the slot the handler wrote, which it dereferenced, and free nothing, since the handler freed what it read, where php's carry `ZEND_JIT_EXIT_FREE_OP1` over a temporary. That bit is in what php's reuse compares (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:181-188`) and moves no exit's number: each of php's two exits holds in its stack map, at the fetch's result, an address of the fetch's own marked `ZREG_ZVAL_COPY` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:8068`, `:8080`), which the fetch gives back before any other exit is asked for (`:8100-8101`), so no other place's exit matches either, as `Stored::Copied` makes the engine's two its own; it shows only as php's exit info's `/FREE_OP1` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:8076-8078`) (revision 140, #3884). A variable of a file's own code (`UnitView::is_main`) or of a function that reads its variables by name is guarded at every read and never narrowed, and a reference's inner type is guarded only where php's code generator fetches the reference, `zend_jit_fetch_reference`, at the oplines `types::opcodes::fetched_reference` lists from its callers, and read as a reference to anything everywhere else, as php's trace SSA types it `IS_UNKNOWN` and adds no guard for it (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:1819-1833`), so an `ECHO`, a comparison or a jump over one takes no exit (revision 128), and its inference reads one as a reference to anything where a guard held it too, since `zend_jit_fetch_reference` narrows the code generator's `op1_info` and no SSA variable's, so what an `ASSIGN` writes through a reference is a reference to anything, `UPDATE_SSA_TYPE`'s widening, and a variable assigned from it holds no type the trace knows (revision 131); an `ASSIGN` to a variable recorded through a reference holds that it is one still, `zend_jit_guard_reference` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:5155-5167`), by an exit at its opline past its value's, and by none where a guard earlier in the trace held the variable's value one, php's `guarded_reference`, which each fetch of the reference sets, the values it writes back carry as `types::opcodes::carried` says, a call that defines no variable of its caller keeps, and a variable a symbol table reaches, every one of a file's own code, never has (revision 129); a temporary the recording saw through a reference, the `VAR` a `function &f()` returns, is fetched where a variable is, at each site but the arithmetic's and those of a variable alone, and a slot recorded through a reference is held to no layout in place, its array's read through the reference or not at all (revision 130). Each opline is `Inline`, the scalar core over the types its guards hold, or `Step` (§5.2). An inline element write, `ASSIGN_DIM` of a scalar by a long key to a variable's array that may be packed (or, revision 95, the array the reference it holds refers to or the indirect `VAR` points at, §4.3's *Arrays*), writes in place where php's `zend_jit_assign_dim` does, the array held once, packed, the key below `nNumUsed` and the element there and counting nothing, and is served by its handler through `step` where php's code calls its helpers, so it takes no exit; php adds no layout guard to an array an `ASSIGN_DIM` was recorded writing packed, so the write tests the layout (revision 77). A compound write to an element, `ASSIGN_DIM_OP` of a number by a long key with `ADD`, `SUB` or `MUL` to a variable's array or the one the reference it holds refers to, is inline as php's `zend_jit_assign_dim_op` compiles it (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:13528-13750`): written in place where `ASSIGN_DIM`'s write would be and the element is a long or a double, a long overflowing to a double, and served by its handler through `step` otherwise, taking no exit, as php's takes none where its record holds no element type, which this tier's recorder does not record; an array read by a long key through a reference is held to its layout at that read alone, `Check::Layout`'s `read` saying it is read through the reference, as php's code generator adds the packed guard to what `zend_jit_fetch_reference` left of it, its trace SSA knowing no type of a reference (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:5830-5880`), and such a guard never moves to a loop's entry (revision 84, #2963). Both the write and the compound write are widened to a hash, to a string key and, for the compound write, to the operators past `+`, `-` and `*` by §4.3's *Arrays* (revision 94). An exit lands at an opline the interpreter serves whole, the one that could not finish or, where it threw, the one that threw; a return to a caller the IP guard did not expect, and a link at a call or a return, leave the opline unset, as php's exit with no opline does. A landing restores the call where compiled code began one and holds it apart from the frame's call chain, as php's code generator delays the link where `zend_jit_needs_call_chain` says nothing before the call may leave: only sends come before it, or, after an `INIT_FCALL`, any opline but a jump or another call's start (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit.c:259-330`; revision 138, #3894). It frees the temporaries a fetch read where a fetched element's guard leaves past the fetch, which it does with a call under way too, the element copied, as php's `zend_jit_guard_fetch_result_type` leaves (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:8067-8085`; revision 101, #3438). The clobber rule reads again, as a new value, every live value a symbol table reaches or that may be a reference; an array the trace owns keeps its layout across a call, since no other code writes it without separating it first. A kernel with no `call` effect that still runs user code clobbers nothing: `INIT_ARRAY`'s and `ADD_ARRAY_ELEMENT`'s handler, whose null key's deprecation reaches an error handler and whose duplicate key releases the value it replaces (`Z/Zend/zend_vm_def.h:6278-6291`), the release kernel compiled code calls for a temporary, which may run a destructor, and the `free_cv` kernel a `RETURN` left in place calls for a variable it frees, after which the caller goes on with every fact it kept (revision 89). What that code can write is a variable a symbol table reaches, which no write narrows and every read guards, a reference, whose inner type no read trusts where no guard holds it, or an array, which it separates first, so no fact the trace keeps is one it can change; php's code generator calls such an opline's handler and keeps every type and register across it (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:6480-6493`) (revision 83, #3306). Revision 26's three omissions are reversed by ADR-0015, built by #3176. What each value holds is read from `php_ssa` where `zend_jit_trace_build_tssa` reads php's analysis of the op array (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:1678-2900`): a value the trace enters with, or a side enters with from its parent's exit, is what reaches its opline met with what the trace knows, `zend_jit_trace_copy_ssa_var_info`; what each opline writes is `php_ssa`'s `zend_update_type_info` over the trace's own SSA, its range `zend_inference_propagate_range`'s and copied from the op array's where the value may be a reference; and each is restricted to what the analysis knows of the op array's value, `zend_jit_trace_restrict_ssa_var_info`. So a long arithmetic whose range stays long takes no overflow exit, the operands of `DIV` and `/=` are typed as php's inference types them and carry no guard of their own (retiring revisions 38 and 60's stand-in), a loop variable php knows the type of is guarded at no entry, and the code generator asks `php_ssa` whether an op's handler may throw, `zend_may_throw_ex`, and whether a read is a value's last, `zend_ssa_is_last_use` (revision 124, #3575). Places that leave with an equal `ExitSpec` naming an opline leave through one exit, as php's `zend_jit_trace_get_exit_point` reuses one whose opline, `flags` and stack map match (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:168-185`), and `ZEND_JIT_TRACE_MAX_EXITS` bounds the exits so taken; a fetched element's guard names the slot its place copied the element into before it left, `Stored::Copied`, so no other place leaves through its exit. The stack map is the stores, a register per slot, and the known types, a slot's type where the value was made one or once a guard before the exit held it, php's `SET_STACK_TYPE`, and a slot not yet read whose entry is one type, as php's code generator sets each such variable's type before the first op, an entry guard's exit knowing those of the variables before it alone and a frame the trace pushes its variables past the arguments unset (revision 124), so successive guards at one place, the entry guards among them, leave through exits of their own, and the entry guards stand in their slots' order, as php's code holds the types the trace entered with slot by slot (revision 48); `Grows::Never` is php's `ZEND_JIT_EXIT_TO_VM`, a loop's interrupt test, numbered after every exit of its body as php takes it where it closes the loop, and a fetch's element it does not find (revision 35; revision 45). An entered trace of a function with typed parameters numbers php's RECV exits ahead of its own: one ZEND_JIT_EXIT_TO_VM exit per RECV that has a type or ends the prologue, as zend_jit_recv takes them for a root frame whose argument count the trace does not know (Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:10836 at 8.5.12), so every later exit, and the side trace grown from it, carries php's number. The prologue ran before Site::Entry (§2.2), so no code tests them; the loop's interrupt test leaves through the first, landing at the entry's opline, as php's reuses it at opline zero (zend_jit_trace.c:7246). (Revision 85, #3269's ruling.) Exits are taken opline by opline as php's code generator takes them: a throw takes none, leaving through an `Unwind` that stores the registers, as php's code jumps to its exception stub; a variable a symbol table reaches is guarded at the oplines php's code generator holds its recorded type at, a concatenation's and a rope part's among them, once php's inference guards the same value anywhere; what an opline over one writes is typed as php's inference types it, over anything and with operator overloading, so the opline that reads it holds it; an arithmetic over one is held where its result is read, as php serves it through its handler; an array of one is held to its layout at each read by a long key, its element twice, read in place and through a reference, where php's analysis says the array may hold one, and once otherwise, `zend_jit_guard_fetch_result_type`'s `deref` (revision 124); and a division, a modulo or a shift by a constant that cannot raise takes no exit (revision 45). An exit is reused across the frames a trace inlines where its spec matches with its frame named by the op array it runs, as php's stack map is relative to the frame pointer; an exit from a frame the trace inlines stores none of its callers' registers, which the call stored to their slots as it entered (`Op::Enter`), since php's stack map, relative to the frame the exit lands in, holds none of a caller's slots, so the levels of a recursion that leave alike leave through one exit (revision 86, #3203); a layout guard's exit is never a type guard's, php's `ZEND_JIT_EXIT_PACKED_GUARD`; an op's exits are numbered as php's code generator takes them, its operands' guards in the order it holds them, `op1`, `op2`, then the `OP_DATA`'s, an `ASSIGN`'s value before its variable (`types::opcodes::checked_order`), then the exit of the way the trace did not go, which php takes before the op's own code, a smart branch's and `FE_FETCH_R`'s before its packed guard (revision 101, #3438), an inline fetch's exit to the interpreter before its layout guard and its element's guards, a call's own exit before its stack check; a table's jump takes an exit at each opline it may go to but the one the trace goes on at, `Check::Arm`, in `zend_jit_switch`'s order; a guard on what the root frame entered with is held at entry only in a trace that loops by its own loop or recursive call, and at its first read in any other, a side's among them, but at entry where php's register allocator loads the value into a register as the trace enters, `ZREG_LOAD`: a long or a double no symbol table reaches, read first by value, whose live range, opened at entry as `zend_jit_trace_allocate_registers` opens it, every opline that reads it keeps in a register, no call the trace follows enters before its last read, and that is read more than once, as php's trace SSA counts its reads (revision 78); what `FE_FETCH_R` writes as its key is typed from the array's layout; and php's stack check, `Check::Room`, holds the stack's room once at a loop's entry for what its calls push and before each `INIT_*` of a function that pushes past what the checks before it cover, leaving for the interpreter (revision 73). A conditional jump whose condition its guards hold as one kind no greater than `true` goes the way the trace went and takes no exit, php's unconditional branch (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:5687-5691`) (revision 76). Where the trace knows the callee of a function's call by its full name (an `INIT_FCALL` or an `INIT_FCALL_BY_NAME`), an argument's send is compiled as php's code generator compiles it once `frame->call->func` is known (revision 82, #2963): a `SEND_VAR_EX` or a `SEND_FUNC_ARG` to a parameter taken by value is a `SEND_VAR`, which leaves its variable's type as it was (`zend_jit_trace.c:2373-2382`), a `CHECK_FUNC_ARG` that finds its argument by value clears the call's by-reference bit inline, and the `FETCH_DIM_FUNC_ARG` after it is a `FETCH_DIM_R` (`:5822-5829`); a method's call on an object whose class the trace holds, `Check::Class`, and whose method is published is pushed in place with `This` as php's `zend_jit_init_method_call` pushes it, and a `NEW`'s call its handler pushes is held to its callee after the handler, `Check::Callee`, as php's `zend_jit_func_guard` holds it, so both send as a function's call does, and the method's frame is left in place, its receiver given up after its variables as `zend_jit_leave_func` gives it up; a dynamic call's sends stay the handlers' (revision 110, #3343). A static method's call over a constant name and a constant class, `self` or `parent`, the three php's trace compiles in place (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:6428-6442`), and a method's call on a request-scoped class, are pushed in place under the pair the opline's runtime-cache slot holds, `CACHE_POLYMORPHIC_PTR(result.num, ce, fbc)`, as php's `zend_jit_init_static_method_call` and `zend_jit_init_method_call` push them (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:9215-9330`, `:9025-9056`): the slot's tag, its function and its class are compared (`tier::PLACE`'s `pair`, `pair_class` and `pair_function`), the class with the receiver's for a method and with a published class's id or none for a static call, and the frame's called scope is written from the slot's class where it is not a constant, and from the calling frame's under `self` or `parent`; a static call to a method that takes `$this` passes the calling frame's where its class is the slot's, as `zend_jit_push_this_method_call_frame` passes it (`:9300-9312`); where the slot holds no pair or another, the handler pushes the call on a cold arm, held to its callee after it as a `NEW`'s is. A request-scoped class's own method, a lazy copy's or an enum's, has a function number of the request's, which no compiled constant names, so the slot's record of it (`tier::PLACE`'s `pair_record`) is compared with the record the request's header image of the class's method holds, as php's `zend_jit_func_guard` holds a function that is not immutable by its opcodes; an evaluated class's method's op array is the request's, and the recording stops where it enters it (§4.1, #3570) (revisions 111 and 116, #3557, #3558, #3561). A `SEND_REF` of a variable holding a reference sends it in place with one owner more, and of one holding none makes the reference in place through the `make_ref` kernel first, `zend_jit_send_ref`, a parameter passed no argument is given its default by a copy, `zend_jit_recv_init`, where the default is a constant no reference counts, a method called on a temporary is pushed in place under its class guard, the call taking the temporary's owner over, `zend_jit_init_method_call`, and a native's or a frameless call is made from compiled code as php's code makes it, the native alone served through a kernel that reads no opline (revisions 110, 117 and 118, #3340, #3560). A `SEND_VAR` of a temporary that is no reference moves it into the call; a `TYPE_CHECK` over a temporary, or a variable that is neither a reference nor unset, is inline, giving up a counted temporary through the release kernel; a `RETURN` left in place hands its caller a scalar, a constant no reference counts, copied, or a temporary, moved (`types::Returned`); and a call to a function that declares its parameters' types is made in place where every parameter is passed and the trace knows each argument is of a kind its declaration holds as it is, the checks `zend_jit_recv` would make each known to pass or made in place against the declared type's mask, an argument that fails one handed to its `RECV`'s handler alone, `zend_jit_verify_arg_slow`, and the rest of the call made in place (revision 117). A call into a frame the trace inlines stores the caller's register-held values to their slots as it enters (`Op::Enter`'s `stores`), as php's allocator keeps no register across one (`zend_jit_trace.c:3169-3180`).

Tested from fixtures: `build` over a parsed record, `lower::dump::render` over the result, diffed against a golden file, and the golden files checked once against php's `TRACE_TSSA` dump for the same programs to fix the guard placement vocabulary; one fixture per clobbering kernel class asserting the reload. The goldens' programs are §11's four, served once on an engine with no extension so their records' view holds their op arrays, since a `UnitView` is built by a request alone (§5.3); the record text is the fixture `build` reads (revision 26).

### 4.3 `lower`: a real seam as a value, and the backend behind it

```rust
pub(crate) struct Compiled {
    bytes: Vec<u8>, entry_offset: usize, exits: Box<[ExitSpec]>, align: usize, leaves: Option<ExitId>, fuzzed: Fuzzed,   // revision 36
    map_ptrs: Box<[MapPtrRaw]>,                  // the runtime-cache slots the code reads, which `enter` lays down (revision 27)
    marks: Box<[Mark]>,                          // code offset at which each recorded opline's code begins; a trace inlines
                                                 // calls, so an opline is named with its function
    name: TraceName,                             // php's `zend_jit_trace_name` but the number, the job's (revision 27)
}
pub(crate) struct Mark { func: FuncRef, opline: OplineIdx, offset: u32 }
pub(crate) struct Backend { /* private: the host ISA, its flags, the kernel symbol table */ }
impl Backend {
    /// Detects the host ISA through cranelift-native and applies the flags `Policy` asks for.
    /// `Unsupported` when the host has no backend; the tier then runs with `Mode::Off` (§4).
    pub(crate) fn new(policy: &Policy, symbols: Symbols) -> Result<Backend, Unsupported>;   // symbols: `kernels!`'s table (revision 28)
    pub(crate) fn compile(&self, ir: &TraceIr, name: TraceName) -> Result<Compiled, Abort>;  // name: the job's (revision 28)
}
pub(crate) enum Kernel { Step, PushCall, Call, Release, Handle, FreeCv, Identical, RopeInit, RopeAdd, RopeEnd, AssignConcat, Concat, Find, Lookup } // the wrappers compiled code calls (revision 28; revision 82; revision 89; revision 92; revision 94)
pub(crate) struct Symbols(/* one wrapper address per Kernel */);
#[repr(C)] pub(crate) struct Stood { frame: *mut u8, call: *mut u8, func: u32, opline: u32, room: usize }   // where a kernel left the interpreter, and the stack's room (revision 73)
pub(crate) const ABANDONED: u32 = u32::MAX;                     // a kernel refused where the opline names no exit
pub(crate) const UNWOUND: u32 = u32::MAX - 1;                   // a kernel threw and the trace stored its registers, taking no exit (revision 45)
pub(crate) mod dump { pub(crate) fn render(ir: &TraceIr) -> String; }
```

The seam is the value `TraceIr`, not a builder trait, and `dump::render` is its second real consumer; a builder trait wide enough to hide Cranelift's `FunctionBuilder` is nearly as wide as Cranelift and is Dax's `LowerHost` again. `Backend` is built from `Policy` and holds the `OwnedTargetIsa` and its flags, so no module but `lower` names a Cranelift type, and a Cranelift version bump is one module's change. `Backend` is `Send + Sync` because it holds no per-compile state, which `lower` asserts at compile time (revision 28); each `compile` builds its own `Context`. The §D fallback, the IR backend, is a second body of `Backend` under a feature, behind the same two functions and the same `Compiled`; an LLVM backend would be a third (`research/llvm-research.r1.md` D.1) and is not built before G8 has a reading.

What the code is. Frame slots are loads and stores at `frame + const`; recorded scalars are `i64`/`f64` SSA values; each guard is `icmp` and `brif` to a cold block that stores the live scalars with their type bytes and returns its `ExitId` (§E.6); kernel calls go through one private emitter, `call_kernel(builder, symbol, args, exit)`, that emits the indirect call to the 64-bit constant, tests the returned `Status`, and branches to the exit block when it is non-zero, or to the opline's unwind block, which stores the registers and answers `UNWOUND`, where a throw is what leaves (revision 45), so a new line in `kernels!` needs no mirrored rule here; where the opline names neither the emitter answers `ABANDONED`, past every `ExitId`, and `enter` reads the fatal or the panic the wrapper kept, as it reads them before it lands any exit (revision 28). The frame chain is the engine's, and compiled code writes a known callee's push, its call and its leave in place over the frame's layout (§5.3, #3222's ruling, revision 79), as php's `zend_jit_push_call_frame`, `zend_jit_do_fcall` with `i_init_func_execute_data` inline and `zend_jit_leave_func` do (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:8466-8650`, `:10016-10600`, `:11035-11263`): an inline `INIT_FCALL` of a user function, or a recursive call, whose push the recording found plain (`tier::PlainCall`) takes its room off the stack's top page, copies the header the request lays out for the call (`tier::call_image`), writes its slots `UNDEF` and its record on the frame table; the `DO_*CALL` the trace follows into it makes the frame in place, writing the activation's flags, entry opline and cache, the caller and the slot that takes the return, and stands the caller at the call, php's `SET_EX_OPLINE`; and a function's `RETURN` whose value is a scalar leaves its frame in place, the `RETURN` a trace stops at among them, freeing first, in slot order, each variable `types` does not hold uncounted as php's trace frees it before `zend_jit_leave_func` (`zend_jit_trace.c:5586-5634`): a type test, then a `GC_DELREF` inline where another owner stands and the value's `GC_TYPE_INFO` holds a bit of php's `GC_MAY_LEAK` mask, and otherwise, the last owner, a possible root of the collector or a reference, the `free_cv` kernel, which takes the frame off the chain a backtrace reads before it releases, as `zend_jit_leave_frame` does, the frees going on past a destructor's throw and the caller then unwinding from its call, php's `jit_stub_leave_throw` (revision 89), after which the interpreter goes on in the caller as it does after php's `zend_jit_trace_return` (`types::End::Return`). Each falls back to the kernel it stands for where the engine's own path refuses the plain case, which is the ruling's guards: a page short of the room, where no stack check holds the push (`types::PushRoom`), or a full frame table at the push; a callee's cache not laid down at the call, whose interrupt is read as php's trace reads it, at a loop's header, past a door and at the next exit (revision 143); at the leave a receiver, a closure, an annex, arguments past the parameters, a variable that owns something in a frame of more variables than the leave frees one by one (`types::Vars`), as php calls `zend_free_compiled_variables` where it does not unroll, a frame a native called or one off the top page, and for the `RETURN` a trace stops at the trace's end exit in place of a kernel. None takes an exit of its own, as php's code takes none there (#3203's measurement), and each writes the `Stood` its kernel would. A native's `INIT_FCALL` whose push the recording found plain is pushed in place as a user function's is, the header the request lays out for it (`tier::call_image`), as php's `zend_jit_push_call_frame` pushes a native's call, and its `DO_ICALL` is made as php's `zend_jit_do_fcall` makes an internal function's call (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:10522-10720`): compiled code stands the frame at the call, php's `SET_EX_OPLINE`, makes the call the one being served by writing its flags (`tier::FRAME`'s `building` and `return_unused`), `EG(current_execute_data) = call`, and hands the `call_native` kernel the call's place and the address of the slot that takes the return, a temporary or, where nothing reads it, a zval on its own stack, php's `alloca`'d one; the kernel serves the native alone, its observer told where one is declared, and reads no opline. After it, with the call on top, compiled code tests the interrupt and checks it through the `interrupt` kernel where it is raised, php's `zend_fcall_interrupt`; pops the call in place where its push was compiled code's, it is the table's last record on the top page, carries no annex and no argument's release can free its value, each counted argument given up by a `GC_DELREF`, and through the `pop_call` kernel otherwise; gives up a return nothing reads; and last tests the throw, php's `EG(exception)` guard; a native that throws is answered with its call popped and leaves at once. A `FRAMELESS_ICALL_*` is made as php's `jit_frameless_icall*` makes it (`:17868-18110`): compiled code stands the frame at the call, reads each operand zval where it stands, a temporary staying in its slot, lays them in the trace's room and hands the `frameless` kernel the native's place in the frameless table, the room and the address of the slot that takes the return, so it reads no opline and the kernel reads no frame (revision NEXT); then frees each temporary where it stands, php's `jit_FREE_OP`, a native's throw left pending through the frees, tests the interrupt as after a `DO_ICALL`, `jit_frameless_interrupt_check`, and the throw. A variable of a frame a name reaches, one with an annex, is held with one more owner through the call, which php's code does not take and the engine's handler does, so a handler that writes it through its name while the native runs leaves the native's copy standing. A variable's reference is read through in place, php's `jit_ZVAL_DEREF_ref`; a variable that may be unset, or a temporary that may hold a reference, is tested first and, where it is, the call is its handler's through the `handle` kernel, which warns of it as php's `zend_jit_zval_check_undef` calls `zend_jit_undefined_op_helper` to (revisions 117 and 118), compiled code writing where the interpreter stands past it where it knows the call left under way, and stepping it otherwise (revision 84). Every other inline `INIT_*` is the `push_call` kernel, which refuses a callee other than the recorded one before it pushes anything, an inline `RETURN` that does not leave in place, and a `DO_*CALL` the trace does not follow into its callee, are `step` (§5.2), and a `DO_*CALL` it follows and does not make in place is `call`, the handler's inline path with no dispatch (revision 65); each writes a `Stood`, where the interpreter stands after it, its frame's room, the room of its call under way, its op array and its opline, by which compiled code follows inlined calls and returns, writes a send to the call's slot, holds php's IP guard and reads the way a stepped jump went (revision 28). `Compiled.bytes` are final and hold kernel addresses as 64-bit constants; a 32-bit host is `Unsupported`. **The interrupt test.** For a trace that ends in `End::Loop` whose header tests the interrupt, every loop but one by recursive return (revision 87), the lowering loads the interrupt pointer from `ExitCtx` once at entry and, at the header, loads the byte it points to and exits when it is non-zero, through the exit `LoopHeader.interrupt` names, at the header opline with `Landing::Plain` (revision 26); the loop then performs the interrupt check under the `Resume` contract (§2.2) and the interrupt helper runs as it would have at the back-edge. Where `LoopHeader.interrupt` is `Interrupt::Stub`, which `types` decides as php's `zend_jit_trace` decides between an exit point and `jit_stub_interrupt_handler` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:7210-7228`), a root that does not loop by returning, whose code php's code generator would run keeping the instruction pointer it entered with (`zend_jit_trace_uses_initial_ip`: an opline php's code generator serves through its handler, `zend_jit_trace_handler`, as its switch decides over what the opline's operands may be, whatever this tier steps or serves inline, or one it compiles inline at the opline the pointer names whose code may call into php's runtime, which stands the frame there first, `jit_SET_EX_OPLINE`, while the pointer still holds the trace's first opline; revision 80) and which holds nothing in registers at the test, the test takes no exit: it answers `lower::INTERRUPTED`, `enter` stands the frame at the header, and `run` makes the loop's interrupt check through `tier::interrupted` and enters the trace again there, as php's stub handles the interrupt and goes on at the header's handler, which is the trace; a throwable a hook left pending resumes the loop to unwind it, and the timeout's fatal bails (revision 51). One load and one branch per iteration, which is php's cost (`rfc-jit.md:118`); a trace without a loop has no test, because it runs a bounded number of oplines before the interpreter's own check, and a recursive trace is bounded by `opcache.jit_max_recursive_calls`. A trace's body is a function on Cranelift's tail convention, entered through one prologue on the host's convention that `enter` calls, php's trace prologue; a link and an exit whose side trace is published each hand on by a tail call to the next trace's body, which lays the entry that trace reads (§4.6) as `enter` lays it and keeps bytes final (§4.4, #3229's ruling, revision 81). Where the jump finds no trace, or the request has not entered that trace through the crossing yet, the exit's number is answered as before and `dispatch` hands on (§4.9). The link tests the interrupt first where php's does, a side trace's link to its own root where the root loops (`zend_jit_trace_link_to_root`, `Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:17418-17432`), and that test and the loop header's are one emitter.

**Strings, and a handler called directly** (revision 92, #3321). An opline `types` serves no inline form for whose handler can neither leave its frame, push or replace one, enter the loop with the frame unstood nor jump (`lower::clif::Onward`, the complement of php's `zend_jit_trace_may_exit`, `Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:261-330`) goes through the `handle` kernel, `tier::dispatch_held`, one dispatch over the held op array with the frame stood at the opline first, as php's `zend_jit_trace_handler` calls the handler and takes no IP guard (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:17129-17236`); compiled code writes where the interpreter stands past it. The three jumps php's code compiles no inline form for, `?:`'s `JMP_SET`, `??`'s `COALESCE` and `?->`'s `JMP_NULL`, go through `handle` too (`Onward::Went`, `Emit::branched`): compiled code reads `EX(opline)` back from the room where the handler left it and holds the opline's direction guards on it, php's IP guard past a branch `zend_jit_trace_may_exit` names (`:17201-17271`). Before the handler, each tests a variable in place where the test reads its type byte and at most one word, as the handler tests it (`Emit::tested`, `Arr::ELEMENTS_AT` for an array's count), and go on with no call; every other value is the handler's, so the trace takes the exits php's takes and no other (revision 115). The string oplines are compiled as php's code generator compiles them: a strict comparison where a string is one side, as `zend_jit_identical` compares it (`:7292-7532`), by the two strings' pointers, then their lengths (`Str::LEN_AT`), then the `identical` kernel, php's `zend_is_identical`, a string and a scalar never identical; `STRLEN` (`zend_jit_strlen`, `:11623-11660`); `BOOL` and `BOOL_NOT` of a scalar; `FE_FREE` of an array (`zend_jit_free`, `:11543-11580`); a concatenation of two strings through the `concat` kernel, php's `zend_jit_fast_concat_helper` and `_tmp_helper` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_helpers.c:1824-1880`), over `ops::concat_strings`, the rule the handler's two arms serve through; a rope's string piece through `rope_init`, `rope_add` and `rope_end` (`zend_jit_rope`, `:11753-11799`, and `zend_jit_rope_end`, `zend_jit_helpers.c:3442-3463`), over the request's ropes, which hold the pieces where php's frame holds them, the piece handed with its owner as the handler's string arm takes it; and `.=` of a string onto a variable's string through `assign_concat`, php's `zend_jit_fast_assign_concat_helper` (`:1788-1822`), over `ops::concat_assign`, which grows the string in place where the variable is its one owner. A kernel that reads no frame and may raise is called with the frame stood in place, php's `SAVE_OPLINE()` at `FRAME.opline()`; one that reads its frame, `handle` and `assign_concat`, stands it where it finds it, as `step` does; and a temporary the handler frees is freed before the kernel's status is tested. A join follows the interpreter's rule (`ops::concat_strings`, `ops::concat_assign`), which hands the other side on where a side that is no constant is empty, where php's helpers allocate always: one rule, so the tier makes the strings and counts the interpreter makes. None of the string kernels runs user code, so none clobbers (§4.2): `types` serves them only where php's guards held the operands as strings, so a piece or a right side that is an object leaves through its guard and its turn is the handler's. The `handle` kernel serves user code where the handler does, a `__toString`, an undefined variable's warning reaching an error handler, the destructor of what an assignment replaced; none of those oplines has the `call` effect, and revision 83's analysis covers each, as it did when `step` served them. A write of a value no scalar to a packed array's element is in place, as `zend_jit_assign_dim` writes through `zend_jit_assign_to_variable` (`:13352-13527`, `:5793-5950`), and a counted value compiled code gives up, an element or a variable written over or a temporary freed, is released as `jit_ZVAL_PTR_DTOR` releases it (`:1819-1903`): a `GC_DELREF` inline where another owner stands and `GC_MAY_LEAK` is false, the release kernel for the last owner, whose destructor is user code revision 83's analysis covers.

**Objects** (revision 93, #3337). A property opline over a constant name, `FETCH_OBJ_R`, `FETCH_OBJ_W`, `ASSIGN_OBJ`, `ASSIGN_OBJ_OP`, `PRE_INC_OBJ`, `PRE_DEC_OBJ`, `POST_INC_OBJ` and `POST_DEC_OBJ` on `$this` or on a variable that holds an object, is compiled as php's code generator compiles one whose `prop_info` it knows on an immutable class. It is compiled on the declared slot the recording took from the engine's cache for the class it saw (`UnitView::place`), under `Check::Class`, php's `zend_jit_class_guard` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:14191-14204`), which compares the object's class at `Obj::CLASS_AT` with a published class's id. The guard is held once per object, `$this` of a frame or a value, as `TRACE_FRAME_SET_THIS_CLASS_CHECKED` and `MAY_BE_CLASS_GUARD` hold it, and the object a property opline writes through keeps its type and class (`zend_update_type_info`, `Z/Zend/Optimizer/zend_inference.c:3028-3083`); any other write to the variable makes a value no guard held. The clobber rule (§4.2) reads again no class a guard held: an object's class never changes, and a value it reads again is a value of its own, guarded again at its first property opline. A request-scoped class, `zend_lazy_class_load`'s copy, an enum or an evaluated class, has an id and slots of the request's, so the same oplines are compiled as php compiles them on a class that is not `ZEND_ACC_IMMUTABLE` (`zend_jit_fetch_obj`, `:14341-14370`; `zend_jit_assign_obj`, `:14874-14895`), each under the one guard on the request's own runtime-cache slot, `Emit::cache_guard`: the slot's address read from the `ExitCtx`, its tag tested before anything past it is read, then the class it names compared with the object's; the place's offset must be a declared slot whose type holds what the opline writes (`tier::PLACE`, offsets `offset_of!` over the interpreter's own `#[repr(u32)]` `exec::Cached` and its `#[repr(C)]` `PropertyPlace`), and its number is read from the slot. Where the guard refuses, a read leaves for the interpreter through the exit it takes for a slot that holds nothing, and a write is its handler's through `handle`, taking no exit, as php's code goes to its slow path; a method's call extends the same guard with its own tag (revision 97, #3346), the slot's pair `ClassAndCallee` (§4.2, revision 111, #3558). The forms:
- **A read** reads the slot at `Obj::SLOTS_AT` and leaves for the interpreter where the slot holds nothing (php's `ZEND_JIT_EXIT_TO_VM`), which is `__get`'s, a lazy object's (whose slots stand empty until it is initialised, so no lazy flag is read, as php reads none) or the warning's. What it holds is copied as `ZVAL_COPY_DEREF` copies it, or held to the type its reader's guards ask, as a fetched element is.
- **A fetch for writing** of an untyped slot writes the slot itself, `IS_INDIRECT`, to its result, as `zend_jit_fetch_obj` writes it, and leaves for the interpreter where the slot holds nothing; a typed slot's is its handler's, as php's is its typed-property paths'.
- **A write** writes as `zend_jit_assign_to_variable` writes, releasing what it wrote over. A typed slot is written in place only with a scalar its type holds as it stands, by the handler's own test (`SlotType::admits`); a `readonly` slot and one of a set visibility the scope lacks hold none.
- **A compound write, an increment and a decrement** of an untyped slot add, subtract or multiply a long, overflowing to a double, or a double in place.
Whatever a write meets that its inline form does not serve, a slot that holds nothing, a reference, a value no number, a typed slot's coercion, is served by the handler through `handle`, taking no exit, where php's code calls its typed-property and `__set` paths. Every property opline no inline form serves goes through `handle` too (`Onward`), `ISSET_ISEMPTY_PROP_OBJ` among them, as php's code calls its handler; fused to the jump after it, it holds its direction guard and is stepped. None of these oplines has the `call` effect: the user code their handlers serve, `__get`, `__set`, a coercion's throw and a destructor of what a write replaced, is covered by revision 83's analysis (§4.2), and the three are witnessed on `Onward::of`. A method's call and `NEW` are stepped as before.

**Arrays** (revision 94, #3339; revision 95, #3370). The dimension oplines over an array a variable holds, the reference it holds refers to, a temporary or, for a write, the zval the `VAR` a fetch for writing left `IS_INDIRECT` points at, by a long key or a string a constant or a variable holds, are compiled as php's code generator compiles them over `zend_jit_fetch_dimension_address_inner` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:11917-12511`): a packed array's slot by a long key in place, where the array's layout guard held it packed or the write tests `HASH_FLAG_PACKED` (php's `MAY_BE_ARRAY_PACKED` and `MAY_BE_ARRAY_HASH` split), and every other element through the table's own lookup, as php's code calls `zend_hash_find_known_hash`, `zend_jit_symtable_find`, `_zend_hash_index_find`, `zend_hash_lookup` and `zend_hash_index_lookup`. Two kernels serve the lookups: `find`, which reads the key as the handler's inline path reads it, a constant string as written and another as `ZEND_HANDLE_NUMERIC_STR` reads it, and answers the element's place or none, reading nothing of the request, and `lookup`, which lays `null` down where a write's key is not there (`tier::lookup_element`, the handler's `array_lookup`). The forms:
- **A read**, `FETCH_DIM_R`, leaves through php's `ZEND_JIT_EXIT_TO_VM` where the key is not there, as the packed read does, and holds the element as before; `FETCH_DIM_IS` writes `null` there and goes on, but where the next opline holds its element's type, it holds the element as `FETCH_DIM_R` does and leaves past itself with the `null` through an exit it takes before the element's two, php's `not_found_exit_addr` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:12576-12610`; revision 114); `isset()` of an element answers whether it is found and, a reference followed, more than `null`, written or fused to its jump (`zend_jit_isset_isempty_dim`, which serves no `empty()`). Each reads a temporary's array as a variable's and frees it after, as the handler's `FREE_OP` frees it and php's `jit_FREE_OP` frees it before the jump, as `zval_ptr_dtor_nogc` gives it up, buffering no possible root: a `GC_DELREF` in place, and the release kernel for the last owner, which runs a destructor of the temporary's element and so is under revision 83's analysis (§4.2). Every temporary compiled code frees is freed so.
- **A write**, a compound write and a fetch for writing, `FETCH_DIM_W` and `FETCH_DIM_RW`, share one path: the array held once, then the slot or the table's element, each layout's path going on to a write of its own, as php's code merges none before it writes. What it does not write in place, a shared or immutable array, a packed hole a compound write reads, a key a compound write does not find and its warning, is the handler's through `handle`, taking no exit; a write's packed slot the array has not got, past `nNumUsed` or a hole, is `lookup`'s, on the table's cold arm, as php's code calls `zend_hash_index_lookup` for it (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:12261-12285`; revision 95). A compound write serves `/` over numbers and `%`, the shifts and the bitwise operators over longs in place, a zero divisor or a negative shift the handler's, which throws, and `.=` of a string onto an element's string through `assign_concat`, which takes the place it writes, a variable's or an element's; php leaves `**` to `zend_jit_assign_dim_op_helper`. A fetch for writing writes the element's place, `IS_INDIRECT`, to its `VAR`, typed `INDIRECT`, php's `MAY_BE_INDIRECT`. A write, a compound write and a fetch for writing over that `VAR` follow it to the zval it points at, as php's `zend_jit_fetch_indirect_var` follows it (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:17087-17141`), under the `Indirect` guard on that zval's type, whose exit goes back to the opline; the `VAR`'s own type is tested only where it may be other than `INDIRECT`, php's `add_indirect_guard`, so past an inline `FETCH_DIM_W` it is not, and a `FETCH_DIM_RW`, whose warning may reach an error handler that frees the array and leaves the `VAR` an error, keeps the test. A `VAR` that points at a reference is followed on through it, as php's `zend_jit_fetch_reference` follows it after `zend_jit_fetch_indirect_var` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:5078-5096`, `:4749-4770`, `:5906-5930`), the same guard holding the zval a reference and the value it refers to of the type it holds, `Read::Indirect`'s `Pointee` saying which (revision 96, #3410); the opline of an increment over a `VAR` is its handler's, as php's code calls the handler of an increment over a `VAR` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:4441`). The one write path resolves its array three ways, the variable's, the reference's and the `VAR`'s, for `ASSIGN_DIM` as for the others.
`Onward` takes the dimension fetches, `isset()` and the increments and decrements, so what a handler still serves of them crosses through `handle`. None of these oplines has the `call` effect: the user code their handlers serve, an undefined key's warning reaching an error handler that frees the array, an `ArrayAccess` method, the destructor of what a write gives up, is covered by revision 83's analysis (§4.2).

**Observed calls** (revisions 11 and 43; design §4.3 seam 9). Compiled code emits observer events where php's JIT does (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_ir.c:4620-4717`): when an observer was declared at engine build, at every compiled call, return and native call, inlined ones included, it reads the callee's resolved slot at run time and calls begin unless the slot reads none-observed, and calls end when the frame is the current observed one; an observed call keeps its frame. When none was declared, nothing is emitted. As built, the read is the interpreter's: where an observer is declared, compiled code pushes, makes and leaves no call in place (revision 79; the recording reads `UnitView::observed`), so it serves every opline that begins or ends a call through its kernel, an inline `DO_UCALL`, `DO_FCALL`, `DO_FCALL_BY_NAME` and `RETURN` among them, and a native's call, a frameless call, a `YIELD` and a generator's return are stepped oplines, so seam 9's homes in the handler (`observer::call`, `returned`, `native_call`, `native_returned`) read the slot behind the engine's one word of how many observers were declared, as the loop's do. The lowering reads no observer state and emits nothing of its own, so an engine with no observer declared compiles the calls in place and a call costs compiled code no observer test.

**Observed opcodes** (design §7.1; revisions 13 and 43). When an extension declared an opcode other than `ZEND_BEGIN_SILENCE` and `ZEND_END_SILENCE`, the tier does not start for that engine, bound `Mode::Off` where the tier is bound, and php's warning is raised through the startup diagnostics, as `zend_jit_check_support` refuses (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit.c:3743-3756`); at those two, compiled code tells the declared opcode's observers before the kernel. When none was declared, nothing is emitted. As built, the tier's engine start asks `tier::observes(start, opcode)` of every opcode but the two, php's `zend_get_user_opcode_handler(i) != NULL` once every extension's start has declared, and `Policy::check_support` runs the tier `Off`, reading no `opcache.jit_debug` bit (revision 39), with `Refused::ObservedOpcode`; the engine's first request raises php's `JIT is incompatible with third party extensions that setup user opcode handlers. JIT disabled.` at `Unknown` on line 0 through `raising::startup`, once, wherever php sets its tier up and so warns, opcache started for the host and `opcache.jit` on or off with a buffer (`Z/ext/opcache/ZendAccelerator.c:3253-3254`), `policy::SetUp::Yes`, and never under `disable` nor where opcache does not start (revision 44). The two silence opcodes are stepped, and `step` tells their observers before the handler, as the loop does.

Targets. `jit` is a default cargo feature on Linux and macOS for x86-64 and aarch64, the two platforms `code` has a `Pages` adapter for (§4.4); "which hosts" within them is answered by `Backend::new`, not by a feature list, and another operating system is a third `Pages` adapter if ever wanted. How Cargo expresses it: `php_jit` is an optional target-specific dependency of `php_bundle`, and the `Tier` alias is gated on `all(feature = "jit", any(the four targets))`, so the feature on a fifth target is `Nop` and not a build failure.

`marks` is what a profiler needs and what a debugger of a bad trace needs: `table` turns it into php's `PERF` map line and `PERF_DUMP` record (§4.5), and `xtask` prints a disassembly annotated by function and opline from the same table. It costs three words per recorded opline in `Trace` and nothing in the code.

Tested at the seam: CLIF text of `compile` for each fixture is a golden file, lowered for x86-64 on System V with the kernels at made-up addresses so the text is one on every host (revision 28); the compiled bytes run under `force` against the oracle; `Backend::new` on a host with no backend is a unit test with a stubbed ISA lookup; the interrupt test is one line of the golden for every loop fixture that takes one, and a recursive return's golden is held to none.

### 4.4 `code`: the arena, sealed by construction

```rust
pub(crate) struct Arena { /* one region of php's buffer's size, mapped twice; chunks of 64 KiB carved inside it, the last what the region leaves (revision 57) */ }
pub(crate) struct Code { entry: NonNull<u8>, len: usize, chunk: Arc<Chunk> }   // entry is in the execute view
pub(crate) type Entry = unsafe extern "C" fn(*mut ExitCtx, *mut Frame) -> u32;

impl Arena {
    pub(crate) fn new(capacity: usize, map_jit: MapJit) -> io::Result<Arena>;
    /// Copies `bytes` into a chunk through the write view, performs the cache maintenance the
    /// target needs against the execute view, and returns a handle into the execute view.
    /// Bytes are final: there is no API to write to a `Code` again.
    pub(crate) fn publish(&mut self, bytes: &[u8], align: usize) -> Result<Code, Full>;
}
impl Code { pub(crate) fn entry(&self) -> Entry; }
```

The arena holds as many bytes as php's buffer, `opcache.jit_buffer_size` rounded up to the page (`ZEND_MM_ALIGNED_SIZE_EX(jit_size, page_size)`, `Z/ext/opcache/ZendAccelerator.c:3257-3263`), carved into chunks of 64 KiB but the last, which holds what the region leaves: a buffer below one chunk is one chunk of its own length, held by the cursor inside it, so it fills where php's fills: php's size is observable, in the dump and in `opcache_get_status()['jit']['buffer_size']`, so design §3.1 has the engine copy it (#3112's ruling, revision 57).
